### Page 1

ASE Technology Holding

3Q26 sales outperformance affirms solid 
transformation progress

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 900.00 TWD | Price: 762.00 TWD

3Q26 sales beat on LEAP ramp and mature ASP strength 
ASE reported 3Q26 sales +28% QoQ better than its guide, driven by stronger IC ATM 
(+20% QoQ vs. BofAe +13% QoQ) and mild upside in EMS (+42% QoQ vs. BofAe +40% 
QoQ). We believe IC ATM strength is led by modest mainstream packaging and testing 
price hike on industry supply tightness, supplemented by LEAP business ramp. EMS also 
benefited from Apple product seasonal builds and higher component cost pass through. 
With improving packaging and testing utilization and ASP and rising LEAP mix, we 
expect its IC ATM GMs to improve 300bps QoQ to 30% (vs. high-20% guide) though 
consolidated GMs (+180bps QoQ to 23%) could be partially offset by EMS outgrowth. 
AI pipeline diversification anchors capex plan in ’26-28 
We model ASE’s 4Q26 IC ATM sales +7-9% QoQ as its LEAP opportunity diversifying 
from TSMC outsourcing to direct project wins including AMD’s Venice CPU, putting sales 
on track to +42% YoY in ’26 (vs. its +35% guide). Its IC ATM GMs could further expand 
to 31% as LEAP reaching 30% of its sales (vs. 18%/22% in 1H/3Q26). While we expect 
ASE to spend US$13bn+ annual capex in ’27-28 (vs. US$10.5bn in ’26), the accelerating 
IC ATM sales (BofAe +40% CAGR from ’26-28) on advanced expansion and mature price 
uplift, along with the business mix upgrade to cloud AI/HPC projects carrying 35-45% 
GMs should offset hiking depreciation (BofAe +39% CAGR from ’26-28), lifting its IC 
ATM GM from 29% in ’26 to low 30% in ’27 and mid 30% in ’28 with operating leverage. 
Stronger earnings quality unlocks re-rating potential 
We lift our ’26/27/28E EPS to NT$20/NT$34/NT$50 on strong LEAP outlook. Keep Buy 
rating and raise POs from NT$800 (US$51) to NT$900 (US$58) still on 20x 2H27-1H28 
P/E (vs. its 5-26x range) backed by its structural business transformation with better 
sales and GMs growth profile as the industry supply further consolidates amid higher 
tech entry barrier.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Established in 1984 and headquartered in Taiwan, ASE 
Technology Holding is the no.1 outsourced assembly and 
testing (OSAT) firm globally, in terms of market share by 
revenue. ASE acquired Siliconware Precision (SPIL) in 2018, 
and had acquired USI in 2010, which became its EMS 
business segment providing synergies in system-level 
packaging. 2 shares = 1 ADR.

Investment Rationale 
We rate ASE Buy, factoring in the company's opportunity in 
advanced packaging and testing evidenced in TSMC's 
outsourcing and its own heavy capex investment. The 
strategy shift should allow the company to capture fast 
growing addressable market in high performance 
computing applications and improves its margins. ASE 
could maintain its leading position in the Outsourced 
Semiconductor Assembly and Test (OSAT) universe, owing 
to its well-established footprints and widespread service 
offerings.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
ASE Technology Holding (XSRIF / ASX) 
We value ASE Technology Holding at NT$900 per share (US$58 for ADR), based on 20x 
2H27-1H28E P/E, above its historical range of 5-26x, in view of its solidified industry 
position in AI era. We use P/E ratio to value ASE as the company has been able to 
maintain healthy profitability through the cycles in the past 20 years due to 
consolidating industry landscape supporting a more benign supply/demand and keeping 
pricing and margins less cyclical.

Downside risks to our PO are 1) share loss and/or ASP erosion owing to Chinese players' 
cannibalization and/or ASE's worsening execution, 2) weakening end demand due to 
uncontrollable matters, such as macro halt and/or geopolitical tensions, 3) regulatory 
issues that could hinder ASE from generating synergies and benefit its competitors.

Improving 3Q GPM helped by better mix — Dynapack's preliminary 3Q26 results 
showed margin recovery yet weak PC sales remained the key drag on the earnings. 
3Q26 EPS of NT$2.33 (+72% q/q and +49% y/y) missed our/BBG est by 45%/27%, 
although we believe the earnings shortfall was well anticipated given the 3Q sales 
miss owing to the delayed shipment of one of the BBU products and weaker PC 
demand. GPM improved q/q on a richer product mix, with higher-margin BBU 
making up c.50% of 3Q sales on our estimate, while OPM recovered to 10.7%, 
+4.2ppt q/q and +1.3ppt y/y. Nevertheless, the sales shortfall constrained op 
leverage, leaving margin improvement insufficient to offset weaker sales. 
PSU vendor shortages weigh on Oct; 12kW ramp to drive Nov sales rebound — 
Our industry checks suggest that Dynapack's major PSU vendor has encountered 
component supply shortages (related to power management IC) and therefore 
delayed BBU pull-ins, weighing on shipments of Dynapack's existing 3kW and 5kW 
BBU products in Oct. We therefore expect Oct-26 BBU sales to decline slightly m/m. 
Meanwhile, PC sales should recover sequentially and more than to offset the BBU 
softness, supporting a slight m/m increase in group sales. Looking beyond Oct-26, 
we expect accelerating shipments of the new 12kW BBU series from Nov-26 to drive 
a strong rebound in BBU sales.

Implications — We reiterate our bullish stance on the BBU sector despite near-term
volatility from external supply-chain constraints, which is likely to put pressure on
near-term sentiment. We view the component-driven delays in product pull-ins
primarily as a shipment-timing issue, with underlying demand remaining strong into
2027E. We also see ample opportunity for Dynapack to gain market share from

Panasonic, its major Japanese peer, particularly as power architectures migrate
toward HVDC. This share-gain potential remains intact, reinforcing our confidence
in Dynapack's medium term BBU growth outlook.

Dynapack International
Valuation
We assign a 32x target P/E to our 2027E EPS of NT$25 to derive a target price of NT$800. We value Dynapack based on 2027E
target P/E as we believe 2027E better reflects its earnings power after BBU capacity expansion. Our target P/E of 32x is based
on our forecast EPS CAGR of 66% in 2025-2027E and a PEG of 0.5x, which we benchmark against its major PSU partner, as we

expect Dynapack to grow alongside its partner.
Risks
Our quantitative model assigns a High Risk rating to Dynapack. However, we believe such a rating is not warranted for
Dynapack due to: 1) the company is generating solid revenue and earnings growth over next 3 years; the underlying industry,
BBU for AIDC, also enjoys secular growth; 2) the business model that involves with product customization and strict
safety/reliability requirements would imply relatively benign competition and therefore stable pricing and margins; and 3) the

company has a strong balance sheet and has been in net cash position since 2019.
Key downside risks to our price target/rating are: 1) Slower-than-expected BBU adoption in AIDC, 2) Delay in PSU customer
qualifications, 3) Competition from larger battery or power solution vendors, 4) Safety, certification and reliability issues.
Panasonic Holdings
(6752.T; ¥3957.0; 1; 16 Oct 26; 15:30)
Valuation
To derive our target price, we use a global sum-of-the-parts (SoTP) approach. In the case of Panasonic Holdings, we reference

52 other companies in the Citi coverage universe to set a fair value PER for each business division, which we sum to derive our
overall target price.
Panasonic fair-value PERs for individual business segments range from 8x to 31x, and have a weighted average of 26.2x
(excluding subsidies in the battery business), taking minority interest and equity in earnings into account. We estimate
business-derived equity value of ¥5,000/share. We calculate the present value of the amount we expect Panasonic to receive

in battery subsidies over five years at ¥400/share. Combining these two values, we derive a target price of ¥5,400.
Risks
Risks to our target price include 1) unsuccessful management policies or strategies resulting in weaker-than-expected
business investment returns or sales growth, or conversely producing greater-than-expected results at a relatively early stage,
2) stagnation in the global economy or an upturn in it, and rises or declines in demand in the electrical industry, and 3) forex

trends. If these factors manifest themselves differently than we have anticipated, the share price may vary from our target
price.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

### Page 1

Nien Made Enterprise

3Q preview: EPS +13% YoY to NT$6.6; 
Better 2H but not yet a turnaround

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 441.00 TWD | Price: 358.50 TWD

3Qe EPS NT$6.6: strong rev and GP offset by opex and FX 
Nien Made is scheduled to report 3Q26 results on 4 Nov. Disclosed monthly sales imply 
3Q26 revenue growth of 16% YoY to NT$8.5bn, or 9% in USD terms, largely 
volume-driven. Growth reaccelerated from 1.7% YoY in 1H26, led by custom-made on a 
low US base and expansion in Europe and Japan. Ready-made also improved as 
Cambodia labor shortages eased. (See Exhibit 1 for US window coverings spending 
trends). Gross margin: We expect underlying GPM to rise 1.1ppt YoY to 59.8% on lower 
tariffs, broadly flat QoQ. We forecasts an NT$300mn of IEEPA tariff refunds in 2H26. 
Assuming NT$150mn is booked in 3Q, this would add 1.7ppt and lift reported GPM to 
61.5%. We expect the selling expense ratio to fall 1.7ppt QoQ to 17.5% on lower fuel 
charges, but remain 0.5ppt higher YoY due to continued salesforce and distribution 
network investment. We expect an FX loss in 3Q26 vs. an NT$118mn gain in 3Q25, 
weighing on YoY earnings growth. We forecast 3Q26 EPS of NT$6.6, up 13% YoY. 
US window covering picking up, but no turnaround yet  
BofA credit/debit card data suggests US spending on window coverings turned positive 
in Aug-26 for the first time after 19 consecutive months of YoY declines. Spending rose 
4%/3% YoY in Aug/Sep, respectively. This improvement partly explains the recent 
reacceleration in Nien Made's monthly sales. That said, we view the improvement more 
as normalization off a low base than the start of an industry turnaround. The Aug-Sep 
2025 comparison base was relatively weak, while the current rate-hike environment 
remains a headwind to housing-related demand. 
Slightly raise FY26 EPS est. to NT$22.9; PO to NT$441 
We raise our FY26 EPS estimate by 2% to NT$22.9 to reflect the blended impact of 
stronger-than-expected revenue growth and FX loss. We maintain our FY26 DPS forecast 
of NT$17 per share. We raise our PO by 4% to NT$441, as we roll forward our valuation 
basis from FY26E EPS to FY27E EPS. At the same time, we lower our target P/E from 
19x to 17x, reflecting the MSCI removal and a less supportive rate environment.

Company Sector 
Interior Furnishings

Company Description 
Nien Made, founded in 1974, is one of the world's largest 
window-covering manufacturers. It has roughly 50% 
market share in both ready-made (OEM business) shades 
and blinds, and custom-made (own brand) shutters in the 
US. The US market accounted for over 75% of sales. Key 
clients include Home Depot and Wal-Mart for ready-made 
shades and blinds. Nien Made operates its own retail 
network and e-commerce business for its custom-made 
shutters under the Norman brand name in the US and 
Europe.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on Nien Made, mainly given 1) clear 
custom-made growth profile (share gain in the US and fast-
growing DTC in Japan and Europe), 2) its largest customer 
Home Depot is gaining market share, and 3) margin 
expansion potentials backed by favorable products mix.

Price objective basis & risk 
Nien Made Enterprise (XNRBF) 
Our PO NT$441 is derived from 17x P/E 2027E EPS. This is around 0.5SD above 
historical the one-year forward P/E, which we think is warranted given the visibility of 
the growth profile.

Downside risks are 1) weaker-than-expected Home Depot's sell-through, 2) slow 
recovery of US housing turnover, and 3) higher tariff increases on China exports to the 
US. 4) Loss of key customers.

貿聯-KY(3665.TT) 
產業：電子–其他電子 
正式合併 Interplex 財報，大幅挹注 HPC 產品營收 
 投資評價與建議 
正式併購Interplex，預估2026/2027年稅後EPS為70.58/128.86元，過往貿聯
本益比位於17~30倍區間，考量併購與光通訊專案逐步發酵，給予25倍PER。 
 重點評論分析 
正式併購Interplex集團Datacom業務，大幅挹注HPC營收：近期貿聯正式合
併Interplex集團資料通訊業務，並自2026年9月23日起納入合併報表。  
Interplex產品包含：伺服器機殼、機櫃、滑軌與CPU Socket等，併購有利提
升貿聯在單⼀機櫃之產值，另外有利切入過往較難打進供應鏈之CSP客戶。 
HPC和半導體設備仍為主要獲利引擎 ， 光通訊佈局逐步發酵 ：展望2026~2027
年 ， 營收動能主要來自 ： (1)Ethernet AEC規格逐漸從400G往800G迭代 ， 800G 
AEC主要採用客戶為xAI與AWS，1.6T AEC預計將於4Q26隨Nvidia Rubin新
平台開始正式出貨，ALC主動式光纜目前已有2個以上客戶表示願意採用，預
估2H27開始貢獻營收 ； (2)Vera Rubin機櫃功率突破300kW ， 帶動Power Whip
規格從60A提升至100A 、 機櫃內Busbar規格從1,400A提升至5,000Ａ ， 電源規
格提升帶動產品單價隨安培數增加而提升；(3)Rubin Ultra機櫃Power Rack設
計帶動Power Whip用量與ASP皆顯著增加 ， 預估產值成⻑2倍以上 ； (4)貿聯與
ams OSRAM合作開發microVCSEL互連方案，另外與SENKO和ficonTEC合作
全自動化光學互連與光纖製備流程，目前光通訊營收已佔整體營收約2~3%。 
 投資風險 
伺服器新平台出貨進度不如預期、原物料成本上漲。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況  
2Q26毛利率重回成⻑軌道，HPC與半導體設備需求持續強勁，3Q26營收創高：2Q26營收季增11.6%至232.6億元，係
因HPC與半導體設備業務需求持續強勁，1Q26毛利率僅28.8%，主要受車用等非AI低毛利產品復甦導致產品組合改變
以及xAI因併購使其資料中心建置進度停滯 ， 導致延後拉貨AEC與power產品 ， 貿聯產能利用率下降亦影響毛利率 ， 2Q26
毛利率季增1.96ppts至30.7%，重回過往水準，係因客戶逐步恢復拉貨，稅後EPS為15.28元。貿聯8月營收月減7.7%至
88.9億元，係因歐洲地區歷經季節性暑期⻑假，9月營收月增5%至93.3億元，營收達成率101%，符合預期，係因xAI逐
步恢復拉貨以及AWS Trainium 3放量，3Q26營收季增19.8%至278.9億元，預估稅後EPS為19.12元。 
正式併購Interplex集團Datacom業務，大幅挹注HPC營收： 貿聯-KY於2026年6月宣布斥資8.5億美元收購新加坡
Interplex集團資料通訊業務，近期正式合併並自2026年9月23日起納入合併報表。Interplex營運總部位於新加坡，產能
遍及南亞與亞洲地區，包括：泰國、越南、⾺來⻄亞與中國等，其核心技術為精密金屬加工與機構件，主要產品包含：
伺服器機殼、機櫃、滑軌與CPU Socket等，另外僅Busbar產品線與貿聯重疊且同為Nvidia MGX合格供應商，併購除擴
張HPC營收規模外，亦提升貿聯在單⼀AI Server機櫃之產值，另外透過併購有利切入過往較難打進供應鏈之CSP客戶。
2025年Interplex營收約4億美元，佔貿聯2025年營收之17%，隨AI運算與推論需求增加，假設Interplex 2026和2027年
營收皆年增30%，預估可貢獻貿聯2026/2027年營收分別約39與200億元。 
HPC 和半導體設備仍為主要獲利引擎，光通訊佈局逐步發酵，成為「銅光並進」主要受惠者：展望 2026~2027 年，貿
聯營收動能主要來自：(1)隨 AI 頻寬需求增加，Ethernet AEC 規格逐漸從 400G 往 800G 迭代，預計 2026 年底前成為
主流規格，800G AEC 主要採用客戶為 xAI 與 AWS，其中 xAI 因 AEC 採用規格最高且⻑度最⻑，故 ASP 較高，1.6T 
AEC 預計將於 4Q26 隨 Nvidia Rubin 新平台開始正式出貨，由於 1.6T AEC 採用 8 條單通道 200G 之線材組裝，故組
裝難度提升且成本翻倍，因此 ASP 和毛利率皆優於過往規格，另外 PCIe AEC 則已開發完成，將隨 CSP 推出 PCIe 版本
Switch 放量，ALC 主動式光纜目前已有 2 個以上客戶表示願意採用，預估 2H27 開始貢獻營收；(2)隨 Vera Rubin 機櫃
功率突破 300kW，帶動 Power Whip 規格從 60A 提升至 100A、機櫃內 Busbar 規格從 1,400A 提升至 5,000Ａ，電源
規格提升帶動產品單價隨安培數增加而提升；(3)Rubin Ultra 機櫃可容納 576 顆 GPU 使單⼀機櫃功耗超過 500kW．由
於機櫃無法容納電源設備，故單獨拉出成為 Power Rack．帶動「Power Rack 至運算機櫃」電源連接需求大幅增加，預
估 Power Whip 之用量與 ASP 皆顯著增加 ， 預估產值成⻑超過 2 倍以上 ； (4)光通訊領域方面 ， 近期貿聯與 ams OSRAM
合作開發 AI scale-up microVCSEL 互連方案，另外持續與 SENKO 和 ficonTEC 合作全自動化的光學互連與光纖製備流
程，目前光通訊營收已佔公司整體營收約 2~3%，顯示光通訊產品成⻑⼒道強勁。綜上所述，預估 2026/2027 年營收
年增 49.5%/55.4%至 1,064.9/1,654.6 億元，稅後 EPS 為 70.58/128.86 元。

旺矽 (6223 TT)  Buy

旺矽營收優於預期 ，CPO 設備出貨將帶動評價提升 ， 給Buy
的投資建議。  
投資建議 ：旺矽營收優於預期， CPO 設備出貨將帶動評價提升，
給 Buy 的投資建議。  
09/2026 營收優於群益預期 ：旺矽 09/2026 營收為 20.48 億元，
MoM-0.02% 、 YoY+82.23% ，累計營收為 150.68 億元，
YoY+58.02% 。實際月營收與群益預估的 16.50 億元，差異為
19.43% ，優於預期。  
3Q26 優於市場預期 ：市場先前預期旺矽 3Q26 營收 QoQ+0~5% ，
實際營收 QoQ+12.76% ，優於預期。  
旺矽有佈局 CPO 測試設備 ：旺矽的 Insertion 3 設備預計 4Q26
向晶圓代工 、IC 設計 、OSAT 送樣 ，1H27 有望進入量產 ；Insertion 
2 設備預計 2H26 會有較明顯的結果。  
旺矽積極擴產： 旺矽表示，目前在許多客戶端都是第一供應商，
供貨比重大多為 80% ，但之前因為產能供不應求，有外溢到第
二供應商，現在有積極擴產。  
旺矽預計 2027 年提高 PCB 的自製率： 探針卡可分為測試頭、
載板及 PCB 。旺矽表示，探針及載板均已全數自製， PCB 預計
2027 年開始量產，目標將 PCB 的自製率提升至 50% 。  
群益預估旺矽 2026 年 /2027 年稅後 EPS 64.86 元 /110.49 元： 群
益預估旺矽 2026 年 /2027 年營收為 214.11 億元 /363.85 億元，

則從事研發、採購、生產、作業及服務等營運活動，以降低公司營運對自然
環境及人類之衝擊。  
旺矽應評估公司經營對社區之影響，並適當聘用公司營運所在地之人力，以
增進社區認同。  
旺矽經由股權投資、商業活動、捐贈、企業志工服務或其他公益專業服務等，
將資源投入透過商業模式解決社會或環境問題之組織，或參與社區發展及
社區教育之公民組織、慈善公益團體及政府機構之相關活動，以促進社區發
展。

上櫃公司訂定道德行為準則參考範例，建置有效之治理架構及相關道德標
準，以健全公司治理。

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 13 日

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

矽光子、海外市場帶動營收持續穩健成長 
 維持買進，目標價 750 元：投顧認為隨汎銓竹北的 SAC-TEM 中心
開始貢獻先進製程 MA 收入，加以 AI 晶片及矽光子檢測需求顯著升
溫加持下，汎銓 2026 年營收成長近三成，毛利率提升至 30%以上。
此外，2027 年矽光子分析設備銷售將開始貢獻營收，估計 2026 年
EPS 4.34 元，2027 年 EPS 15.28 元，維持買進，目標價 750 元。 
 預期 3Q26 毛利率大幅優於前期 ，EPS 1.81 元：汎銓 3Q26 營收 7.59
億元 ，+19.6%QOQ，+31.6%YOY， 投顧估計毛利率將大幅優於2Q，
3Q26 EPS 1.81 元。 
 AI、矽光子、海外市場、矽光子分析設備等多動能齊發，預期 2027
年營運明顯走升：汎銓今年每月營運成本大致穩定在約 2.1 億元內，
27 年每月營運成本約 2.16 億元，只要營收持續成長，獲利將迅速放
大。為因應 AI 需求，AI 專區第三期擴產於 7 月完成並開始貢獻營
收，且計畫在年底前開始第四期擴產，對下半年業務樂觀看待，尤其
海外業績成長極為迅速 ， 預計到今年底 ， 海外業務佔比將有望達將近
40%。另外，汎銓已成功自主研發並組裝完成 3 台矽光子分析設備，
其核心光損偵測裝置技術已順利取得台灣、日本、美國及韓國專利。
標準版設備 9 月開放客戶驗證，11/5 正式對外實機展示，今年底開
始銷售，預期今年首批有 2~3 台設備可出貨，明年目標出貨 10 台，
標準機台售價約 6,000 多萬元 ， 此設備毛利率預期在50%以上水準，
若額外選配各項高階測試模組，單台售價可提升至 1 億至 3 億元。

汎銓 主力為 MA （材料分
析 ） ， 營 收 佔 比超 八 成  
 汎銓成立於 2005 年 7 月，為檢測分析服務公司，協助客戶解決先進製程中遇到
的研發與品質問題，以提高客戶的生產效率，尤其在 AI 晶片、先進封裝、矽光
子領域有深厚布局 ，此外由於材料分析檢測交期平均在三天內、 故障分析檢測交
期在一周內，成為其競爭優勢。以營收比重來分，材料分析（MA）佔 2026 前 7
月營收比重 84％，IC 故障分析（FA）營收佔比 8％、矽光子+AI 晶片營收佔比
8％。 
汎銓有九個 MA 實驗室，三個 FA 實驗室，一個 RA 實驗室。2Q26 台灣營收佔
比 65%， 美國 、 日本 、 大陸等海外營收佔比35%， 目前全球員工人數共869 人。

預期 3Q26 毛利率 大幅 優於 前
期，EPS 1.81 元 
 汎銓 2Q26 營收 6.35 億元，+9.8%QOQ，+16.6%YOY，隨 AI 晶片檢測分析等
高毛利業務需求強勁，毛利率回升至 24.96％，季增 3.4 個百分點，費用率降至
22.4%， 稅後EPS 0.35 元 ， 營業利益金額 1611 萬元 ， 稅後淨利金額 1860 萬元。
汎銓 3Q 營收 7.59 億元，+19.6%QOQ，+31.6%YOY，投顧估計毛利率將大幅
優於 2Q，3Q26 EPS 1.81 元。

AI 、 矽 光 子 、 海 外 市 場 、矽
光 子 分 析 設 備 等 多動能 齊
發 ， 預期 20 2 7 年 營運 明顯
走升  
 汎銓表示今年每月營運成本大致穩定在約 2.1 億元內，27 年每月營運成本約
2.16 億元，只要營收持續成長，獲利將迅速放大。今年 3 月起各個新建廠區已
開始貢獻業績，公司運營已從 3 月開始轉虧為盈。為因應 AI 需求，AI 專區第
三期擴產於 7 月完成並開始貢獻營收，且計畫在年底前開始第四期擴產 ，對下
半年業務樂觀看待，尤其海外業績成長極為迅速，預計到今年底，海外業務佔
比將有望達將近 40%。投顧預期汎銓 4Q26 營收季增 8.6%，年增 39.2%，估
計 4Q26 稅後 EPS 2.8 元。 
另外，汎銓已成功自主研發並組裝完成 3 台矽光子分析設備，其功能即是協助
客戶進行光損及漏光定位，並開發從晶片背面進行收光偵測的技術，鎖定矽光
子常用的 1310nm、1550nm 波段，協助定位 PIC 元件、光耦合及封裝過程中
的異常位置，且其核心光損偵測裝置技術已順利取得台灣、日本、美國及韓國
專利。對汎銓更具戰略意義的是，將不再侷限於檢測分析服務供應商的角色，
而是應客戶強烈需求，規劃進一步推出適用於量產端與品質驗證使用的矽光子
測試設備。標準版設備 9 月開放客戶驗證，11/5 正式對外實機展示，今年底開
始銷售，預期今年首批有 2~3 台設備可出貨，明年目標出貨 10 台，標準機台
售價約 6,000 多萬元，此設備毛利率預期在 50%以上水準，若額外選配各項高
階測試模組，單台售價可提升至 1 億至 3 億元。汎銓近年積極建立 AI 晶片分析
平台，並同步發展矽光子檢測、自主設 備開發與專利授權等新成長動能，期望

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용)

3Q26 Preview: 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 전망 
3Q26 매출액 1조 3,380억원(+6.4% yoy, +1.3% qoq), 영업이익 5,890억원(-
7.0% yoy, +0.4% qoq, OPM 44.0%)으로 컨센서스에 부합하는 실적을 전
망함. 3분기 평균 원/달러 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락하고, 지난 5월 
파업에 따른 생산 차질 영향이 반영되나, 5공장 PPQ 물량의 매출 인식이 
시작되며 yoy, qoq 외형 성장은 이어질 것으로 예상함.  
파업 당시 생산이 지연된 물량은 3Q 중 추가 생산되어 4Q26부터 매출에 반
영될 전망임. 5공장은 다수 프로젝트의 PPQ가 진행 중이며, Ramp-up에 
따라 매출 기여도 점진적으로 확대될 것으로 예상함. 이에 4Q26에는 파
업 이연 물량 반영과 5공장 매출 확대에 따라 실적 성장폭이 확대될 전
망임. 2026년 연간 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 5조 5,310억원(+21.4% 
yoy), 2조 4,780억원(+19.8% yoy, OPM 44.8%)으로 전망함.

신규 수주 회복 기대, 중장기 성장 기반은 확대 중 
관세 관련 불확실성으로 둔화됐던 고객사와의 수주 논의가 재개되고 있음. 
10월 2일 유럽 소재 제약사와 935억원 규모의 CMO 계약을 추가 체결
했으며, 4Q26 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목함.  
PolyPeptide 인수는 연내 거래 종결을 목표로 진행 중이며, 인수 완료 
시 펩타이드 분야로 CDMO 포트폴리오가 확대될 전망임. 6 공장도 연내 
착공을 목표로 준비 중임. 
한편, 노사 리스크는 단기 불확실성 요인임. 노조는 10/26부터 3주간 2
차 총파업을 예고함. 1차 대비 예정된 파업 기간이 길어진 만큼 실제 파
업 진행 시 생산 차질 규모가 주요 변수임. 다만 파업 개시 전까지 협상 
가능성이 열려 있어, 향후 노사 협상 진행 경과를 확인할 필요가 있음.

산업 투자의견 
- Overweight(비중확대) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 초과 상승 예상 
- Neutral(중립) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률과 유사한 수준 예상 
- Underweight(비중축소) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 하회 예상

기업 투자의견 
- Buy(매수)  
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상 
- Marketperform(시장수익률) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상 
- Underperform(시장수익률 하회) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 하락 예상

### Page 1

2026 年三季度以来，全球市场巨震：
股市⽅⾯， AI 科技股遭遇年内第⼆轮剧烈调整。 10 ⽉初 OpenAI 披露年化收⼊接近 500 亿美
元，远低于市场预期的 700 亿美元，引发 AI 概念股⼤规模抛售。费城半导体指数连跌 8 个交易
⽇，创 2008 年以来最⻓连跌纪录；纳指单周累计下跌 4.48% ，为 6 ⽉以来最⼤单周跌幅；英伟
达 9 个交易⽇市值较⾼点蒸发约 0.6 万亿美元。韩国股市由上半年全球最强市场转为重灾区，
KOSPI 指数 10 ⽉ 6 ⽇以来连跌 5 ⽇、累计跌幅近 15% ，创 8 ⽉以来新低。A 股科技成⻓显著回
调，创业板指从 6 ⽉ 9 ⽇的 3961 点回落⾄ 10 ⽉中旬的 2963 点，上证指数⼀度失守 3700 点，⽩
酒、银⾏等红利消费板块逆势⾛强。港股恒⽣科技指数年内跌幅仍超 25% ，点位接近 2024
年 “9·24” ⾏情区间。
债市⽅⾯，美债收益率全线飙升。美联储 9 ⽉ 16 ⽇宣布加息 25 个基点⾄ 3.75%-4.00% ，为

2023 年 7 ⽉以来⾸次加息，点阵图显⽰年内仍有⼀次加息。 10 年期美债收益率从 7 ⽉中旬的
4.55% 左右⼀路上⾏， 10 ⽉ 7 ⽇升⾄ 5.365% ，创 2002 年 4 ⽉以来新⾼； 30 年期美债收益率升
⾄ 5.732% 。中债则延续低位震荡， 10 年期国债收益率在 1.68% 附近，中美 10 年期利差倒挂约
356 个基点，处于历史⾼位区间。
⼤宗商品⽅⾯，中东冲突再度升级，美伊在霍尔⽊兹海峡附近冲突加剧，胡塞武装袭击沙特
能源设施，油价重回 100 美元 / 桶上⽅， 10 ⽉中旬 WTI 原油单周再涨超 10% 。贵⾦属则⾼位震
荡：⾦价⾃ 2026 年 1 ⽉创历史新⾼后最⼤回撤约 25% ， 7 ⽉⼀度回落⾄ 4000 美元 / 盎司附近， 8
⽉反弹⾄ 4600 美元 / 盎司， 10 ⽉以来⼜回落⾄ 4100 ⾄ 4200 美元 / 盎司区间，年内由涨转跌。
美债、美股、⻩⾦、 A 股的定价逻辑都在剧烈重构，踏准节奏的投资者有效规避了回撤，追⾼热

⻔板块的则损失惨重。为了帮⼤家看清 2026 年四季度⼤类资产轮动与⻛向变化，我们邀请了百
亿私募敦和资管⾸席经济学家徐⼩庆，于 10 ⽉ 25 ⽇周⽇晚 19:00 担任 Alpha 线上闭⻔私享会主讲
嘉宾，分享他对四季度宏观⼤类资产配置⻛向的最新研判。

### Page 2

10 ⽉ 25 ⽇周⽇晚上 19 点，百亿私募基⾦敦和资管的⾸席经济学家徐⼩庆主讲的Alpha 线上闭⻔私
享会，将会分享他最近研究关于股票、债券、商品、外汇、⻩⾦等⼤类资产的最新变化，研判
当前宏观⾛势与 2026 下半资产配置⽅向，看清背后的配置逻辑与关键时机，助⼒个⼈的关键决
策。
这是徐⼩庆今年第三次做客 Alpha 闭⻔会。
2 ⽉的分享：在⻩⾦⽩银暴跌后，他提出贵⾦属将从单边上涨进⼊震荡格局，并提⽰美股居⺠
参与度已超互联⽹泡沫时期、市场脆弱性显著上升。
7 ⽉的分享：他将半导体与年初的⽩银⾛势类⽐，提⽰ AI 科技股可能正在构筑阶段性顶部，同
时判断⻩⾦进⼊⾼位震荡、 4000 美元 / 盎司以下具备配置价值。
此后数⽉的市场演绎印证了上述多项判断。站在四季度的新起点，美债利率为何突破 5% ⼤幅上
⾏，何时迎来拐点？ A 股⻛格切换是否已经开启？⻩⾦何时王者归来？这些议题都将在本次分享
中得到详细展开。
徐⼩庆曾任中⾦公司⾸席债券分析师、董事总经理，带队 4 次当选新财富固定收益研究 “ 最佳分

析师 ” 第⼀名，连续 10 年⼊围前三名。在⼗年间持续获得掌管数万亿资产的⾦融机构集体投票⽀
持，徐⼩庆是中国 “ 最懂利率 “ 的⼏个⼈之⼀。2013 年，徐⼩庆加⼊百亿私募基⾦敦和资管担任
⾸席经济学家、宏观策略总监，并担任敦和宏观对冲系列基⾦的投资经理。
20 多年的从业经历⾥，从卖⽅研究最佳分析师到百亿私募的买⽅投资⼈，徐⼩庆将对中国利率
变动影响资产轮动的前沿研究，⽤于投资实践指引资产配置。早在 2018 年，华尔街⻅闻就邀请
徐⼩庆打造⻅闻⼤师课《⼗年利率⼼法》。在课程中，徐⼩庆⽴⾜中国市场，总结出⼀套⾏之有
效的中国特⾊ “ 利率为锚 ” 的资产轮动与配置⽅法。这是他从业 20 多年间，亲历中国债券市场 5 轮
⽜熊轮动，对中国利率周期影响资产轮动进⾏系统跟踪研究的成果。这⻔课程⽬前已获得上万
名⽤⼾付费订阅，⼀直是⽤⼾评价最⾼和回看复习最多的课程。
每次 Alpha 闭⻔分享也特别安排了 30 分钟的问答互动环节，参加的会员朋友可以针对⾃⼰最关⼼
的话题，获得嘉宾的答疑解惑。对闭⻔分享活动感兴趣的朋友，可以点击下⽅活动海报报名加

⼊ Alpha 线上闭⻔私享会会员，⼀起来听听他对市场的最新洞察。

Document 273: CoreWeave会是2026年的硅谷银行吗？

Source PDF: all report pdf/CoreWeave会是2026年的硅谷银行吗？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

巴克莱认为，规模仅 20 亿美元的⽆担保债券遭抛售，并不意味着 AI 融资链条断裂。相较于
五⼤云⼚商今年预计⾼达 7250 亿⾄ 8000 亿美元的 AI 资本开⽀， CoreWeave ⽀出占⽐仅约
4% ⾄ 5% 。真正的⻛险在于算⼒租赁价格下跌，侵蚀企业现⾦流并引发信⽤⻛险扩散。
摘要：
CoreWeave ⽆担保债券收益率飙升⾄ 15% ，重新点燃市场对 AI 基础设施投资周期能否持续的担
忧。但巴克莱认为，投资者可能正在重蹈 2023 年硅⾕银⾏危机时的覆辙，将⼀家公司的局部信
⽤⻛险误读为整个⾏业的系统性危机。
据英国《⾦融时报》，巴克莱全球研究主席 Ajay Rajadhyaksha 指出，CoreWeave ⽆担保债券遭
遇抛售，主要反映其在公司资本结构中处于较低的偿付优先级，⽽⾮ AI 资本⽀出周期出现裂
缝。他认为， Chamath Palihapitiya 和埃隆 · ⻢斯克等⼈过度解读了这批规模仅 20 亿美元的⽆担
保债券所释放的信号。
从市场表现看，此次波动的冲击远不及 2023 年硅⾕银⾏危机期间的全⾯恐慌。纳斯达克 100 指数

上周下跌 5.5% ，本周已有所反弹，其他超⼤规模云计算商的债券收益率也随着美债市场企稳⽽
有所回落。⽬前，市场尚未出现 AI 基础设施融资全⾯收缩的明确信号。
⽆担保债券收益率飙升，不等于融资链条断裂
CoreWeave 的融资结构主要包括有担保贷款、可转换债券和⽆担保债券，三者的偿付优先级与
⻛险差异显著。公司主要通过独⽴特殊⽬的载体（ SPV ）发⾏有担保的延迟提款定期贷款
（ DDTL ），以 GPU 硬件、客⼾合同及数据中⼼租约作为抵押。今年 3 ⽉发⾏的 85 亿美元 DDTL
4.0 ，以 Meta 多年期 “ 照付不议 ” 合同为⽀撑，利差为 SOFR 加 225 个基点，穆迪评级为 A3 。
但 CoreWeave 的融资成本正在上升： 5 ⽉发⾏的 31 亿美元 DDTL 5.0 ，利差扩⼤⾄ SOFR 加 450 个
基点； 8 ⽉发⾏的 26 亿美元 DDTL 5.5 ，利差进⼀步升⾄ SOFR 加 550 个基点。与此同时，公司过
去⼀年累计发⾏约 90 亿美元可转债，票息为 1.75% ⾄ 2.875% 。尽管 9 ⽉⼀笔可转债发⾏规模从

30 亿美元上调⾄ 37 亿美元，但股价下跌正给这⼀融资渠道带来压⼒。
此次遭到抛售的⽆担保债券则处于资本结构末端。 Rajadhyaksha 指出，清算时，这批债券之前
仍有数百亿美元的优先索偿权，因此 15% 的收益率更多反映其⾃⾝的⾼⻛险，⽽⾮整个⾏业的
融资状况。
他以房贷和信⽤卡作⽐：信⽤卡利率⾼，并不意味着整个城市的房贷市场都出了问题。融资成
本上升值得警惕，但不能据此认定 AI 基础设施融资链条已经断裂。
CoreWeave 体量有限，难以撼动万亿美元 AI 投资周期
即便 CoreWeave 陷⼊困境，其对整体 AI 基础设施投资的影响也可能相对有限。 Rajadhyaksha 指
出，公司 2026 年资本⽀出指引为 350 亿⾄ 390 亿美元，⽽微软、 Meta 、 Alphabet 、亚⻢逊和甲⻣
⽂五⼤超⼤规模云计算商今年的 AI 基础设施⽀出预计合计达 7250 亿⾄ 8000 亿美元， 2027 年更有
望突破万亿美元。
按这⼀⼝径， CoreWeave 的⽀出仅占⾏业总量的约 4% ⾄ 5% 。即使公司完全停⽌⽀出，整体 AI

资本⽀出也只会减少约 4% ⾄ 5% ；若⽀出减半，影响则约为 2% 。Rajadhyaksha 认为，即使按
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
照 2027 年⾏业⽀出超过万亿美元计算， CoreWeave ⽀出减半所造成的缺⼝也约为 200 亿美元，
不⾜以终结整个投资周期。
市场担忧的另⼀层⻛险是信⽤传染： CoreWeave 若出现问题，是否意味着⾕歌等超⼤规模云计
算商也将⾯临融资困境？ Rajadhyaksha 认为，这⼀推论跨越了太多环节。微软、 Meta 、
Alphabet 和亚⻢逊拥有更强的⾃由现⾦流⽀撑，对⽆担保债券市场的依赖程度也较低。
⼀家⾼度依赖外部融资的算⼒供应商出现信⽤⻛险，并不意味着整个 AI 投资链条都将陷⼊融资困
境。
真正的⻛险信号：算⼒价格是否崩塌
Rajadhyaksha 指出，真正值得警惕的不是 CoreWeave 的债券收益率，⽽是算⼒价格是否⼤幅下
跌。 CoreWeave 等 AI 基础设施公司的商业模式，本质上是购⼊ GPU 、出租算⼒，以租⾦收⼊覆

盖融资成本、设备折旧和运营⽀出。⼀旦算⼒租赁价格下跌，收⼊不⾜以覆盖成本，企业的盈
利能⼒与偿债能⼒就可能同步恶化， AI 投资周期才会⾯临更实质性的挑战。
⽬前，供应链数据尚未明确指向这⼀情景。台积电的业绩反映芯⽚需求仍在增⻓， ASML 的订单
积压显⽰相关设备需求依然强劲，超⼤规模云计算商的资本⽀出计划也尚未显露明显退缩。
CoreWeave ⾃⾝披露的合同积压订单⾼达 1040 亿美元，其中包含 “ 照付不议 ” 性质的合同，为未
来收⼊提供了⼀定⽀撑，但并不能完全消除融资与履约⻛险。
Rajadhyaksha 认为，从 CoreWeave 债券收益率升⾄ 15% ，直接推导出 AI 基础设施融资已经断
裂、资本⽀出周期即将结束，缺乏充分依据。本周市场的反弹也暂未印证最悲观的判断。不
过， AI 交易已成为左右市场⾛势的重要⼒量，投资者对任何可能动摇这⼀叙事的信号保持⾼度
敏感，并不意外。
CoreWeave 会不会成为 2026 年的硅⾕银⾏，关键不在于⼀批低优先级债券的收益率有多⾼，⽽

在于融资压⼒是否会进⼀步传导⾄算⼒价格、企业现⾦流和整个⾏业的资本开⽀。  巴克莱认
为，市场可能⾼估了此次债券抛售所代表的⾏业⻛险，但算⼒需求与租赁价格能否持续，仍是
判断 AI 投资周期⾛向的关键。

Document 282: De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-09-15
Pages: 17
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
De-reservification, Not De-dollarization
The world isn’t moving away from the dollar. It is shifting its dollars from traditional
reserves to state banks, pension funds, and other quasi-sovereign investors.
(Bernardo Montoya/Courtesy Reuters)
PUBLISHED
September 15, 2026 8:37 a.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
This is a joint blog with Alex Etra, Senior Macro
Strategist and head of Flow of Funds at Vanda
Research.
The IMF’s data on the currency composition of
foreign exchange (FX) reserves is scrutinized carefully
for any signs that the world is shifting way from the
dollar.
Yet it doesn’t really matter that much if the dollar’s
share of reserves has shifted from 56.5 percent to 57
percent. The ink-to-impact ratio of the quarterly

reporting on the latest COFER data is all off.
Neither the stock of global reserves nor the stock of
dollar reserves has changed much in the last ten years.
The action is elsewhere.

### Page 3

3
China’s state banks (per the Bank of International
Settlements) have almost as many foreign assets as the
central bank (PBOC).
Japan’s Government Pension Investment Fund
(GPIF) has almost as many foreign assets ($986
billion) as the government has FX reserves ($1.1
trillion at the end of August). Japan’s FX reserves are
on the books of the Ministry of Finance and the
GPIF is overseen by the Ministry of Health, Welfare
and Labor—so these are almost all assets of the

Government of Japan or the broader public sector,
not its central bank.
Korea’s National Pension Service has more foreign
assets than the Bank of Korea has FX reserves.
A different dynamic is in play in Taiwan, as the
government doesn’t run a big retirement fund. But in

### Page 4

4
a world where the hedge ratio of the life insurers has
been (and still is) used as a policy tool, the shift in
Taiwan’s foreign asset accumulation away from the
Central Bank of China (CBC) and over to
institutions whose activities in the foreign currency
market it directly influences broadly fits into the same
theme. So too does the PBOC’s use of the
“macroprudential adjustment factor” effectively adjust
banks’ net foreign assets in much the same way that

CBC regulations adjust lifer hedge ratios.
In the last year, the lifers’ hedge ratios—and their
direct hedges with the central bank—have been one
of the key tools the CBC has used to manage the
Taiwan dollar (and guard the lifers’ solvency).
Asia’s big surplus economies are in a sense just
catching up with the world’s oil exporters. Norway
put its oil surplus into a sovereign wealth fund from
the start. Kuwait, Qatar, and Abu Dhabi (the most
oil-rich emirate) all also have large sovereign funds.

And the world’s biggest oil exporter has joined them:
The Public Investment Fund has almost as many
foreign assets as the Saudi Central Bank (SAMA).*
What’s more, the available evidence suggests that the
dollar share of these pools is in line with or higher
than the dollar share of global FX reserves (it depends
on the institution and jurisdiction).

### Page 5

5
In other words, by looking at the (easily available)
data on the world’s static holdings of formal FX
reserve assets, scholars and analysts miss most of the
growth in the world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign
foreign assets.
Consider China.
No serious analyst now disputes that China’s state
banks—including the policy banks (the China
Development Bank, China Exim)—hold several
trillion in foreign assets.
China reports $3.3 trillion in gross foreign assets to

the BIS (the net position, counting foreign bank
claims on the entire Chinese economy not just the
banks, is $2.5 trillion). That maps to the BOP data,
which shows almost $4 trillion in gross outflows
through the banking system (technically, the sum of
gross outflows in “other” plus the $500 billion in
foreign currency bonds held by the state commercial
banks)

### Page 6

6
Since 2010 (and even more so after 2014) the net
outflow through the state banks has exceeded reserve
accumulation.
The broad contours of this story are confirmed by the
balance sheet data reported by state commercial
banks in their 2025 annual reports, which showed

### Page 7

7
that the top five banks held a combined $2.5 trillion
in foreign currency assets (mostly held abroad).
The Chinese haven’t disclosed the foreign assets of
the two policy banks (with at this stage the complicity
of the IMF, which has neither analyzed the role of
SAFE policy bank financing, nor highlighted the
glaring gap in China’s own reporting). But the work
of AidData points to nearly $1 trillion in foreign
assets, with a hefty dollar share.
The available data sources all suggest that the bulk of

banks—puts the dollar share of their offshore foreign
currency assets at around 70 percent. That is well
above the last disclosed dollar share of China’s formal
reserves (55 percent in 2019). The banks’ annual

### Page 8

8
reports suggest that about 70 percent of the banks’
assets are in dollars and Hong Kong dollars (55
percent USD/15 percent HKD)—a bit below the
dollar and Hong Kong dollar share of their liabilities
(swaps out of the HKD fund a decent share of the
banks euro assets).
Put simply, SAFE’s static dollar holdings aren’t the
important story.
The real story is the rapid growth of the state banks
dollar holdings.
Japan holds a high (though undisclosed) dollar share

in its FX reserves, and those reserves are primarily
invested in Treasuries—so Japan’s MoF is now clearly
the largest contributor to the U.S. data on foreign
official holdings of Treasuries. (SAFE has shifted its
funds out of U.S. custodians, and thus increasingly
appears in the data as a “private” holder in a European
custodial center)
Japan’s formal reserves haven’t changed much in the
last ten years; periodic sales to limit the yen’s weakness
have offset most of the MoF’s accumulated interest

income. Foreign currency reserves were $1.2 trillion
in 2014 and $1.16 trillion at the end of Q1. They
dipped to about $1 trillion at the end of August after
sales of close to $100 billion at the end of July/start of
August.

### Page 9

9
What has grown is the foreign portfolio of the GPIF.
it was around $400 billion in 2014, ~$1 trillion in Q1
of 2026.
GPIF’s foreign bonds aren’t held exclusively in dollars,
but roughly 52 percent of its foreign bonds (26
percent of all bonds) are USD-denominated.
But the GPIF, like most international investors, holds
a large share of its equity portfolio in the U.S. (~$165
billion, 65 percent of the foreign equity portfolio).
Korea is a more extreme version of Japan.

The Bank of Korea’s FX reserves have been roughly
$375-450 billion over the last ten years or so. Around
two-thirds of those are now in dollars (as of the end of
2025).

### Page 10

10
From 2012 to 2025, the foreign assets at the NPS
have grown from ~$80 billion (around 21 percent of
total assets) to over $660 billion (55 percent of total
assets)—and something like 70 percent of those are in
dollars. 76 percent of the fund’s equities are in dollars,
and 66 percent of its debt. And while a lower share of
its “alternative” portfolio is in North America, most
alternative mandates are awarded in dollars.

### Page 11

11
In other words, the majority of the dollar assets held
by Korea’s government (the NPS reports to the
Ministry of Health and Welfare, and the Minister of
Health chairs its asset allocation committee) aren’t
held by the central bank and aren’t in the IMF’s
COFER data set.
Taiwan is an unusual case, as the life insurers aren’t
technically sovereign investors. But with their
regulator now explicitly encouraging the them to hold
an unhedged foreign currency book and hence they

now rely on the state for their solvency, they have a bit
of an official character (a significant appreciation of
the TWD would blow through their volatility reserves
and leave them with fewer assets than liabilities, as
their foreign assets are largely held against TWD
policies). The CBC certainly now uses the lifers desire
to unwind existing hedges to help manage the Taiwan

### Page 12

12
dollar. And they have an exceptionally high dollar
share in their foreign assets—over 95 percent.
The Saudi Public Investment Fund (PIF) is another
example.
It keeps roughly $200 billion of its $250 billion in
foreign investments in dollars—well above the U.S.
share of a standard global stock index. Almost all of
the PIF’s $30 billion in external debt is also in dollars.
And the PIF now has only slightly fewer foreign
assets than the Saudi Central Bank has reserves.**

The broad story is thus pretty clear: the growth in the
world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign assets is not
coming through an increase in FX reserve holdings
managed by the world’s central banks.

### Page 13

13
And, in the critical case of China, the dollar share of
China’s quasi-sovereign foreign assets is likely higher
than the dollar share of their formal FX reserves.
Adam Tooze has noted that inflows into the U.S. are
now coming from investors looking for a profit, not
investors looking for safety. What he calls the “profit
dollar” manifests itself in a number of different ways.
More inflows into corporate bonds and equities for
one. Less official demand for Treasuries for another.

More opacity too.
Those same trends are at play among sovereign and
quasi-sovereign investors.
So don’t obsess about the dollar’s chare in formal FX
reserves. Do recognize that the dollar’s “reserve
currency role” isn’t the source of any significant new
inflows into the dollar.

### Page 14

14
The dollar’s dominant role in the international
monetary system depends on much more than the
size and composition of central bank FX reserves. It is
as much, perhaps more so (given that FX reserve
managers are themselves ultimately liability matchers)
a function of the portfolio choice of a set of
private/semi-private/quasi-sovereign investors that
are much more difficult to observe. Reserve currency
status is not only about FX reserves in a strict sense.

That’s been true for some time. Most of the current
flow into U.S. from the “official” sector is coming
from investors who are not classic reserve managers.
Those flows remain heavily tilted toward the dollar, at
least for now.***
And any real de-dollarization would likely occur first
among these investors.
Put differently, the dollar’s global role is increasingly
as a source of returns, not a source of safety. The
foreign bid, private and public, is for risk, not for

Treasuries. That doesn’t help Scott Bessent much
right now, but it has helped keep valuations in the
stock market extended. And the global debate on the
dollar’s role lags the evolution in the dollar’s role, and
the risks associated with that new role.
It is currently fashionable in some circles to point to
the diminution of the “convenience yield” on

### Page 15

15
Treasuries even while the U.S. dollar continues to
enjoy such a privilege. But the shift in the global
investor base, and their apparent portfolio preference
for U.S. risk assets in lieu of Treasuries, may help
explain the divergence between the convenience yield
on UST and the convenience yield on USD.
And of course, if the negative convenience yield
implies that Treasuries are trading at a historical
discount, the persistence of the latter—and its

concentration in risk assets—suggests that the “profit
dollar” itself is likely trading at a historical premium.
That apparent premium on U.S. risk assets deserves as
much (or more) scrutiny as the currency composition
of central bank FX reserves.
* The PIF and Saudi Aramco also now have a certain
amount of debt.
** Reserves (now mostly in Treasuries, so should
appear in the TIC data, unlike at some times in the
past.

### Page 16

16
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.

CAPITAL FLOWS BANKING
*** Japan is the main exception; the GPIF’s dollar
share is below the likely dollar share of Japan’s MoF.

Document 303: Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us

Source PDF: all report pdf/Five ways the UK’s Autumn Budget could shock us.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

From better ﬁscal headroom to surprise income tax hikes, this
is how UK Chancellor John Healey's ﬁrst budget could surprise
investors
James Smith
UK Chancellor John Healey
In a nutshell: What we expect
Higher debt interest costs look set to drive the level of 'ﬁscal
headroom' from £24bn in March to £10bn. There's a growing
sense that Chancellor John Healey would like to get this up
to £15bn or higher. The government is set to modestly
increase spending on the cost of living, though the total

intervention is unlikely to meaningfully top £5bn and it's
likely to be mostly billed as temporary to avoid counting
against the main ﬁscal rule. We expect an increase in capital
spending of roughly £10bn/year, bearing in mind this is
exempt from the ﬁscal rule dictating that the current budget
must be in surplus after ﬁve years. To rebuild headroom

### Page 2

against that rule, we expect £5-6bn of tax hikes, likely via a
higher Bank Surcharge and possibly broadening the National
Insurance tax base.
In short, we don't expect the budget to deliver a material
stimulus (or add materially to the already-planned ﬁscal
consolidation). The impact on the Bank of England, which is
poised to hike rates in November, is likely to be fairly
minimal.
But all chancellors like to keep a rabbit in their hat – and what
follows are ﬁve ways it could surprise us.

1. Fiscal headroom goes up – not down
What if the UK’s ﬁscal hole isn’t nearly as bad as we think?
The widespread expectation going into the UK’s Autumn Budget on
28 October is that the Chancellor is going to have to face up to
higher debt interest costs with some modest tax hikes.
Bank of England rate expectations are almost a percentage point
higher than they were at the time of the Oﬃce for Budget
Responsibility’s March assessment of Britain’s public ﬁnances. 10-

year bond yields are some 70 basis points higher, which, taken
together, will push up debt interest spending by £11bn/year by
2029/30.
That points to a sizeable reduction in the amount of money the
government has left under its main ﬁscal rule, which requires the
current budget (day-to-day spending, including debt interest less
revenue) to be in surplus within three years. Back in March, that
surplus, or ﬁscal headroom, was projected at £24bn in 2029/30.

Debt interest costs are rising

*Based on the average rate in the 10 days up to 25 September, which
likely represents the OBR's observation window for this budget
Source: Macrobond, ING
We think that headroom will have dropped to around £10bn before
any corrective action. And the vibe from the Treasury suggests the
Chancellor would prefer it to be above £15bn, to give more
breathing space in case of future adverse forecast revisions. That
would point to some limited tax hikes.
But what if that’s all wrong? The truth is, nobody really knows how

the OBR will change its assessment of the UK economy. And as we
saw last year, the Treasury is often inclined to talk up the doom and
gloom to open up a positive surprise on the day. 12 months ago,
the hit to headroom was considerably smaller than had been
widely expected.
This time, any positive surprise is most likely to come from wage
growth. Will the rise in energy prices spill into higher pay, just as it
did in 2022? We think it won’t, not least because the jobs market is

much weaker, an assessment we suspect the OBR will share.
But even relatively small upgrades to wage growth can make a big
diﬀerence to the government’s fortunes. Since 2010, the OBR has
revised the level of earnings across its ﬁve-year forecast by an
average of 1.3 percentage points in absolute terms. If it did that
now, say by lifting wage growth over the next two years by 0.6ppt
apiece, it would add £10bn to headroom, almost perfectly
oﬀsetting the hit from higher interest costs.

### Page 4

There are also reasons to think the impact of lower migration
projections, which some reports suggest could wipe another £7bn
oﬀ headroom via lower tax revenue forecasts, could be much less
than feared. The starting point for employment looks a little higher
than it did back in March. The rise in global equities this year should
also be helpful for capital gains tax projections.
Why does this all matter? Because the higher the headroom, the
less ‘need’ there is to hike taxes, and if it is signiﬁcantly higher than

expected, the more room there is to increase spending under the
ﬁscal rules.
The simple message is this: don’t judge the Autumn Budget by the
level of headroom left over.
2. Spending splurge
The problem with ‘headroom’ is not only that it’s highly sensitive to
the OBR’s economic outlook, but that the forward-looking nature of
the ﬁscal rules opens the door to a lot of creative accounting.
The impact of higher inﬂation is a case in point. This helps the

Treasury through higher receipts – both VAT and income tax. Yes, it
also pushes up the cost of indexed beneﬁts. But importantly,
departmental budgets don’t automatically increase to oﬀset the
impact of higher prices.
That opens up a classic trick from the Treasury, which is to leave
spending plans virtually unchanged in nominal terms, setting up
substantially smaller real-terms budget increases across the
medium term.
Even before the Iran War, government plans pencilled in real-terms

cuts to per capita departmental spending in FY2029. Given some
functions, like health, are ‘protected’, that implies much deeper cuts
to smaller departments.
Suﬃce it to say, that is unrealistic. Higher inﬂation will push up the
wage bill across government. It would cost £12bn to simply
maintain those March departmental spending plans in real terms.
That’s before considering the cost of ending that austerity pencilled

### Page 5

in for later this decade, or how the government will fund its defence
or social care ambitions.
A top-up to government departments is inevitable – the question is
when. It looks like the Treasury wants to kick the can on these tricky
budget decisions until next autumn, when it plans to release its
next Spending Review, a process of allocating money across
departments. No doubt the hope is that debt interest costs will
have fallen and the economic outlook brightened, such that there's

more space under the ﬁscal rules to make bigger spending
commitments.
That points to a very modest package of spending measures this
month, mainly targeting the cost of living. On top of the already-
announced cut to VAT on energy, we’d imagine the Treasury will
replicate last year’s budget and lower household bills by funding a
greater share of ‘policy costs’ from general taxation. This has the
added beneﬁt of lowering headline inﬂation at a particularly

sensitive moment for the Bank of England. Crucially though, the
government is likely to bill these moves as temporary, so they don’t
count under that main ﬁscal rule.
But if the economic picture really is better than feared, don’t rule
out the government going further. A decision to fully fund the UK’s
3% defence spending commitment would be a major wildcard,
though this would likely come at the expense of capital plans in
other areas of government.

Ultimately, the key thing to watch is the OBR’s assessment of how
all these policy changes aﬀect GDP growth and borrowing over the
next year. If there’s a material stimulus eﬀect, it could add to
market bets on UK rate hikes.
3. A manifesto break on income tax
Tax hikes are likely at the Autumn Budget, but should be very small.
We’re probably looking at a package of circa £5bn (0.2% of GDP).
Part of the problem is that the list of viable options looks perilously

small if Labour is to avoid breaking its manifesto pledge not to raise

the major taxes, which together account for around 70% of total

Our best guess is that the budget will include a hike in the Bank
Surcharge (raising £2bn if increased from 3% to 8%), together with
a broadening of the tax base for National Insurance to landlords
and partners in LLPs (together yielding around £2bn/year). Other
options, like ending a loophole on stamp duty for commercial
property or lowering the VAT threshold for businesses, have also

been on the table.
The problem with many of these measures is that they require
massive changes to stand any chance of raising material sums,
risking big behavioural reactions while oﬀering uncertain revenue
potential. There’s a reason why former chancellor Rachel Reeves
shied away from many of the measures we outline in the chart
below.
How the Chancellor could raise taxes

Is there scope for a surprise? We think there are two main ways on
tax – one good, one bad.
The good? A surprise announcement of a so-called ‘tax switch’, a
novel proposal from Resolution Foundation which would see the
government simultaneously raise basic income tax and cut

employee National Insurance by 2ppt. The impact on workers
would be neutral, but because income tax has a broader base
(including pensions), this would raise £6bn/year.
Though not a substantial sum, it would signal to investors that
Prime Minister Andy Burnham isn’t afraid to go near the thorny
issue of raising taxes. But in light of recent pension changes,

### Page 8

including to the infamous ‘triple lock’, this feels like too much of a
political gamble at this stage.
The negative surprise would be if the Treasury decided to raise the
basic tax allowance. Burnham ﬂirted with this on his ﬁrst day in
oﬃce; it would not only be expensive (potentially £10bn), but it
would also signal that the UK’s commitment to the threshold freeze
– which is doing the lion's share of the planned ﬁscal consolidation
– is waning.
A big rise in the National Living Wage

The minimum wage increase in April 2027, which will be
announced alongside the budget, is perhaps the most important
variable for the Bank of England.
This is based on a recommendation from the Low Pay Commission,
which earlier this year provisionally estimated the increase at 3.7%.
That’s a smaller increase than over the past two years and,
crucially, is not a million miles above the level the Bank considers
consistent with a 2% medium-term inﬂation target (3.25%).

But the LPC has some ﬂexibility in its recommendation, and one
criterion is the cost of living. Higher inﬂation means we could see a
ﬁnal proposal between 4% and 4.5%. Anything above that would,
we suspect, be problematic for the Bank of England and would
bolster the case for higher rates.
The government is no doubt well aware of this. And there appears
to be growing recognition among Labour ﬁgures, including on the
left of the party, that the big minimum wage increases, coupled

with previous payroll tax increases, have had a big impact on
business – particularly in consumer services.
But if the LPC were to recommend minimum wage growth of close
to 5%, could the government resist it? In theory, yes. But politically,
we suspect not.
A surge in bond issuance
We already know that bond issuance is set to increase again in
2027, though for reasons unrelated to the UK’s ﬁscal trajectory.

something investors wouldn’t take kindly to.

Document 309: FX Daily_ AI jitters have damaged USD safe haven appeal

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ AI jitters have damaged USD safe haven appeal.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

After big moves in front-end USD rates, the dollar has shown it
could be a laggard in further stock market instability if led by AI
concerns. Still, we don’t think we are on the brink of a major
USD decline. EUR/USD may struggle to stay supported today as
protests in France spill over into local assets, while we see
limited impact from US-Ukraine news for now

Francesco Pesole and Frantisek Taborsky
Markets will be closely monitoring AI, France and Ukraine this week
USD: AI jitters leave USD on less solid
ground
AI-related jitters entered the FX picture last week, although
currencies are more accurately borrowing the reaction from the
rates channel. The USD two-year OIS/SOFR has dropped 25bp from
the 14 October post-US CPI print. These are mammoth moves
considering they have been driven almost entirely by AI concerns,
with eﬀectively no macro input.

### Page 2

Markets appear to have a clear reaction function in mind: stock
market turmoil is enough to drastically reduce Federal Reserve
tightening plans, even with inﬂation running hot. The reasoning is
probably linked to the market wealth eﬀect that many believe has
underpinned US economic resilience. This is enough to suggest the
dollar will not be the safe-haven of choice and may actually lag
many other G10 currencies if stock market instability originates in

tech/AI.
Still, contagion to broader US and world equity indices has so far
been contained, while the Nasdaq drop has only erased the last 30
days of gains. AI-related sentiment will remain an input for FX this
week, but could easily fade if tech stocks ﬁnd some stability. From a
macro perspective, despite the repricing lower in US rates, we do
not feel this marks the start of a major USD downtrend. A December
Fed hike remains a likely outcome; policymakers do not appear to

be in a position to send a dovish message at the October meeting,
and still-elevated energy prices argue against a material recovery
in global bond markets.
A return to 101.0 in DXY remains possible over the coming weeks,
but we do not see much more downside beyond that for now. The
US calendar is rather light this week, while the FOMC is in its pre-
meeting communication blackout. For now, we are not observing
any obvious FX impact from souring US-Ukraine (and Europe)

relationships. Markets are probably quite cautious about pricing in
an imminent end to the Russia-Ukraine conﬂict or any longer-term
implications for Europe. Things could change in the case of
meaningful developments this week.
Francesco Pesole
EUR: French protests may curb upside for
now
EUR/USD brieﬂy dropped back below 1.120 overnight before inching
higher again this morning. The move in short-term rate diﬀerentials
has pushed our short-term fair value estimate for EUR/USD higher

to around 1.137-1.139. The current undervaluation is therefore
around 1.3-1.5%, broadly consistent with French ﬁscal risk

Canada releases inﬂation data for September today. The headline

particularly hot, especially given core measures have remained
anchored around 2.0%.
The jobs market has given some warning signals, with two
consecutive negative prints in August and September and the
unemployment rate rising to 6.5%. Still, with growth proving
resilient and a widening rate spread with the Fed, an (insurance)

CEE: Hungary to release its budget plan

Today’s focus is Hungary’s draft budget for next year. Hungarian
bonds have rallied strongly in recent weeks on growing
expectations that the plan will target a deﬁcit below 5% of GDP.

However, much of the good news now appears priced in, creating
downside risk if the government falls short of expectations. Even so,
the broader outlook for bonds remains positive, particularly
compared with this year’s 7.0-7.5% deﬁcit, and we expect investors
to buy any dip following a sell-oﬀ.
The region’s ﬁrst economic releases arrive on Tuesday, with Polish
wage, PPI and industrial production data. The main focus, however,
will be the National Bank of Hungary meeting. We expect rates to

remain unchanged at 5.50%, leaving attention on forward
guidance. Although the central bank has identiﬁed the ﬁscal plan
as a key factor for future easing, we believe cuts remain premature
and, global conditions permitting, are unlikely before the end of this
year or next year.
On Thursday, we expect the Central Bank of Turkey to resume its
easing cycle for the ﬁrst time since the US-Iran conﬂict began,
cutting rates by 100bp to 36.00%. September’s inﬂation data

provided a positive signal, while developments in investment funds
point to downside risks for the economy in the ﬁnal quarter.
Although crowded long TRY positions oﬀer less and less carry, they
remain popular in emerging markets, and we expect this demand
to persist in the coming months despite renewed rate cuts.

developments in Romania, where the new government appears
highly likely to win a conﬁdence vote. This should help stabilise

Document 369: The Commodities Feed_ Sustained Middle East flows fail to dent oil prices

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Commodities Feed_ Sustained Middle East flows fail to dent oil prices.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 3

### Page 1

Oil prices remain ﬁrmly supported, with ICE Brent hovering
around $110/bbl as markets stay focused on persistent risks to
Middle Eastern supply

Warren Patterson and Ewa Manthey
Source: iStock
Energy – China continues to draw down
crude inventories
Oil prices remain well-supported amid ongoing tensions in the
Middle East despite relatively strong Persian Gulf ﬂows. This
suggests the market is pricing in a signiﬁcant risk premium, given
the situation's volatility. However, if ﬂows continue uninhibited
despite the geopolitical environment, the risk premium would likely
erode slowly. Clearly, there are no signs of this yet.
Another major issue in the oil market is logistics. Namely, the
record tanker freight rates we are seeing, given the less eﬃcient use
of tankers due to developments in the Middle East. The US is
considering steps to allow the sale of sanctioned tankers, which

would increase tanker capacity in the legitimate market. The idea is
to bring freight rates down and reduce the delivered cost of oil to

China released output data yesterday showing that domestic crude
oil production rose 0.6% month-on-month and 2.5% year-on-year
to 4.45m b/d in September. Meanwhile, reﬁneries processed a little

over 14m b/d of crude over the month, up 0.4% MoM but still down
8.4% YoY. This is the largest amount of crude processed by Chinese
reﬁners since March. Along with previously released trade data, this
suggests that China drew down crude oil inventories at a pace of a
little more than 500k b/d, or 16m barrels through September. The
numbers also suggest that apparent demand fell by 2.7% MoM and
15.2% YoY to 12.75m b/d in September.
Metals — China aluminium supported by

strong smelter margins
Data from China’s National Bureau of Statistics (NBS) showed
primary aluminium production increased 3.1% YoY to 3.9mt in
September, supported by high smelter operating rates and healthy
proﬁt margins amid global supply disruptions. Cumulative output
rose 3.8% YoY to 35mt during January-September 2026, keeping
annual production on course to approach the government’s
capacity cap of around 45mt.
Meanwhile, crude steel production edged up to 73.6mt in

September. Demand, however, remained subdued due to the
prolonged weakness in China’s property sector. As a result, year-to-
date crude steel output is still down 2.9% YoY to total 724.8mt in
the ﬁrst nine months of 2026.
Agriculture — India ethanol policy eases
sugar supply concerns
Sugar prices fell for a second consecutive session, touching
US¢20.5/lb. This followed India's suspension of sugar-based
ethanol purchases by state-owned fuel retailers, easing concerns

◼ AI眼鏡朝更輕薄、高整合發展，不
僅軟板使用數量提高，硬板亦導入
更高線路等級與mSAP製程，PCB技
術門檻顯著提升，預期AI眼鏡營收
在2026/2027年皆保持快速成長。

### Page 22

全球生產基地加速擴產，效益將於2026年起逐步顯現
◼ 泰國一廠：
◼ 主要生產高階iHDI、HLC及光模塊產品，於3Q25進入量產。
◼ 量產爬坡順利，已通過多家客戶認證及量產，尚有多家伺服器與光模塊全球領先客戶正積極進行認證中。
◼ 7月已達損益兩平。
◼ 目前另有三棟新廠房及一棟機械鑽孔中心同步建置中，規劃將於4Q26起逐步進入試產階段。
◼ 淮安園區：
◼ 規劃至2026年底，淮安HA、HB、HC園區將有6棟高階PCB新廠完成建設。
◼ 另HD園區已於今年四月動土，規劃新建HDI/MSAP與HLC廠，8月10日封頂，預計明年三月加入生產。
◼ 深圳園區：
◼ 第三園區智慧製造基地於今年七月動土，預計興建三棟智慧製造廠房，重點布局AI伺服器用及光模塊用的
高階類載板，及AI終端用高階軟板等高端產品。
◼ 禮鼎深圳第二園區於今年九月正式動土，總投資金額不低於人民幣120億元，規劃建設數座工廠，聚焦
FCBGA等高階IC載板。新產能主要面向AI、高效能運算及先進封裝需求，進一步強化公司高階FCBGA產品的

研發、製造與規模化交付能力。

Markets)，亦於2022年至2025年連續4年入選標普全球永續年
鑑 (Sustainability Yearbook)。
► 臺灣公司治理評鑑
2025年公司治理評鑑排名上市公司6%-20%，再度入選臺灣
證券交易所公司治理100指數。
► Sustainalytics ESG 風險評級
最新Sustainalytics ESG風險評級為16.1，為低等風險等級。
► ISS ESG 評級
最新年度的ISS ESG評級為「C+」，獲得「優秀 (Prime)」
等級。
► CDP 碳揭露專案
2025年在氣候變遷與水安全管理皆獲得「A」最高等級殊
榮，其中氣候變遷為首度獲得A級，水安全則已連續兩年獲

得A級。
► 富時羅素 FTSE Russell ESG
最新年度的FTSE Russell ESG評級獲得4.5分（滿分為5分），
成為台灣PCB企業第一名。

### Page 3

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
Markets have had little respite over the past month. Hopes 
of a lasting US–Iran agreement have faded and intermittent 
attacks on shipping have kept Brent oil prices in triple digits. 
Workarounds have allowed a meaningful share of crude to 
continue flowing but these arrangements are slower and 
costlier, while depleted inventories leave little room to 
absorb another shock and a range of disruptions to refining 
and shipping are also impacting downstream energy costs. 
Bond markets have been just as restless, with continued lifts 
in government bond yields across much of the globe and 
more sharply in the US. 
Notably, oil is no longer doing the heavy lifting for yields. 
The rise has been concentrated in real yields, with the Fed’s 
September hike – and its signal of more to come – acting as 
something of a trigger. In the US, fiscal concerns have been 
more of a sub-plot, though the Treasury’s efforts to hold 
down long yields have arguably only drawn more attention 
to them. 
In our view, markets are getting ahead of themselves on 
monetary policy offshore. We expect just one more hike 
from the Fed rather than the three the market currently has 
priced, with stretched housing affordability posing risks to 
both consumption and inflation. Central banks in Europe, the 
UK and Canada are also likely to bide their time after a final 
move. Europe is weathering the energy shock better than 
feared and has far more fiscal room to respond than the US – 
one reason we still see the US dollar trending lower over the 
next two years.
The RBA hiked again in September and looks set to follow 
up in November, taking the cash rate to a new cycle high. 
Some of the risks the Board flagged have crystallised, 
but the minutes also reveal a tendency to reach for the 
more hawkish read of the data, in addition to the familiar 
downbeat take on supply capacity. Our base case assumes 
no return to full-scale conflict, but with energy buffers 
depleted, bond markets less forgiving and the RBA already 
leaning hawkish, the margin for error – for markets and 
policymakers alike – is starting to ‘wear thin’.
Australia: After a soft but better than expected first half, the 
Australian economy is facing into more challenges as another 
round of disruptions from global energy markets combine with 
further interest rate rises and a deepening housing downturn. 
The demand mix should see capacity pressures continue to 
ease but with significant risks on either side.
Middle East: Low inventories and higher shipping costs will 
keep oil and fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast 
to average US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by 
Q4 2027 as supply recovers. Risks are two-sided: escalation 
could push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran 
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply.
Commodities: Most commodities rose in September, with 
many of the themes that drove moves across August remaining 
in place. Japanese LNG and Brent oil recorded strong gains 
amid Middle East tensions. Copper and aluminium extended 
recent gains while iron ore was supported by higher freight 
and production costs. Gold fell after the Fed’s September rate 
hike. 
Global FX Markets: In late September and early-October 
the DXY index popped higher to near 103, a high back to 
early-2025. Underlying the move are expectations that 
inflation will prove persistent globally as well as a belief that 
the US’ tech sector leaves it best positioned to weather the 
storm.
New Zealand: Higher fuel costs and a weaker exchange rate 
have lifted the short-term inflation outlook while the economy 
has continued to grow through the Iran war. Growth looks set 
to continue to be buoyed by strong commodity prices and a 
supportive exchange rate. However, downside risks are growing 
from higher energy costs and long-term interest rates. 
United States: The US housing market remains under 
considerable pressure. Prices continue to edge up, supporting 
wealth. But up-and-coming main street consumers are 
struggling with affordability and availability, posing enduring 
risks for both consumption growth and inflation.   
China: With each month that passes, the risk of disappointing 
authorities’ 4.5-5.0% ambition grows. While the Government 
is right to take a targeted approach to support, it must quickly 
multiply its scale if downside risks are to be kept at bay. 
Asia: For much of the past twenty years, Asia has been a 
source of disinflation for the world. But a combination of ageing 
populations, rising labour costs, increasing electricity demand 
and technology sector developments imply Asia may provide 
less support to global disinflation over the decade ahead.
Europe & UK: The euro has come under considerable pressure 
during the past month. That said, the European economy 
continues to tick along nicely while inflation risks remain 
manageable, allowing the ECB to be measured in their response.

Wearing thin

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
